ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u18	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! For many years, Polish families were not cared for, thinking that the family would be able to cope with the realities of the free market. However, the family is not an economic entity - it is a living organism, and it adapts much longer to change, and it can be more painful to feel it. The negative situation of the family affects the condition of the whole nation. This is a far-reaching perspective, but the demographic crisis, which is the result, among others. The state's neglect of the family is already affecting Poland, and it has been for many years. Only the current government - the government prime minister Beata Szydło... [[A lie, a lie!]] ...the government of Law and Justice - appreciated the family, recognized that the family was the foundation of the nation and should be taken care of. Our government openly says that investing in the family is an investment in the future of the nation, the best investment, an investment with a high rate of return, and that the most important value of the nation is family. On behalf of the Government, Prime Minister Beata Szydło, I would like to present to the High House a bill amending certain laws relating to family support schemes. A year ago, I presented to the High House a draft law on State aid to raise children, a family of 500+. It was a groundbreaking programme of family policy. I already announced to you, the High House, that, in cooperation with the communities of practitioners, experts, local authorities and local authorities, we will review the instruments that are currently functioning in Poland in terms of their effectiveness in order to introduce and develop a strategic comprehensive action plan to improve the lives of families who raise children. Today is the time for our announcements to be realized more than a year ago. The proposal for new regulations is the next point of development of an effective and effective family policy to encourage Polish families to have more children. The draft law amending the existing regulations is the result of the government's review of family support schemes in April. These amendments concern the following laws: the Law on State aid in raising children, the Law on family benefits, the Law on assistance to persons entitled to maintenance, the Law on the care of children under the age of 3 years and the Act on the Charter of the Large Family. The main objectives of the scheme remain unchanged. We focus on eliminating undesirable phenomena. The assessment of the impact of the introduction of the 500+ family programme so far is clearly positive. The programme pursues the three basic objectives that have been set before it. These are investments in the family, poverty reduction among children and young people and the pro-natalist objective. Thanks to the program, many families finally felt safe and finally felt that the state appreciated their efforts and dedication. Many families say that thanks to the program she regained her dignity, raised her forehead. For this reason, it is not proposed to change the basic principles of the "Family of 500+" programme, so it will continue to be a non-taxable benefit of 500 PLN paid per child up to 18 years of age. In the case of income under PLN 800 or PLN 1200 - in the case of a disabled child in the family - the benefit is also payable to the first child in the family. So these changes that we are proposing to you in this bill will concern the elimination of a few, albeit undesirable phenomena. What were they? Well, they consisted, among other things. on the adjustment of income by some parents to the income criterion which entitles them to provide education to the first child and to declare that they are not in line with the fact that they are single parents and that they are not resident in Poland. I will refer to this first element: excluding the application of the income loss mechanism for a period of 3 months. The answer to this first irregularity is the proposal to exclude the application of the income loss mechanism in the event of loss of employment or other employment, including non-agricultural economic activity, by the person concerned and the re-employment of that person within a short period of time - 3 months - in the same employer, employer or contracting entity, or the re-starting of non-agricultural economic activities and to change the way in which the income of non-agricultural economic activities to be determined is to be taxed on the basis of the provisions on flat-rate income tax on certain income generated by natural persons. On the basis of a study by the Institute of Labour and Social Affairs, the Minister will announce revenue by means of a notice. Another mechanism we want to change, too. Well, we want to eliminate misconceptions about raising a child alone by requiring child support from a second parent. It will be possible to eliminate fraud related to non-true statements of single parenting by requiring child support from a second parent, since, in accordance with the constitutional principle of subsidiarity, child care should only be granted to a single parent after the possibility of obtaining maintenance from the second parent has been exhausted. May I remind you that the same solution has been in place for more than 12 years in the case of the granting of a family allowance, and for these 12 years it has been implemented in this form. We propose to accept this as a solution also in the case of educational provision. [[No way. Something good was before.]] Another issue is the verification of the right to provide for the place of residence and the obligation to provide personal explanations. According to the Law on State Aid to Raising Children, an educational benefit is payable if the applicant or recipient resides in Poland for the period during which he is to receive an educational benefit. Hence, among others. changes that we propose in the field of verification of the fulfilment of the conditions which entitle us to provide education, and in particular the condition concerning residence in Poland, because this benefit is not entitled to migrants who have permanently left Poland and who reside abroad. The competent authority paying the benefit in case of doubt may call on the person receiving the benefit to provide adequate personal explanations. The payment of the parental benefit shall be suspended if the person receiving the benefit refuses to grant or does not give an explanation within the prescribed time limit. In case of clarification, the parental benefit will be paid from the month in which the explanations were received until the end of the period during which the decision was taken and during which the parental benefit is entitled. At this point, I would like to stress that, at the same time, we are equiping local authorities with very important IT tools, which will allow for verification of the condition of residence of applicants for or recipients of benefits. This applies to both parental and family benefits as well as maintenance fund benefits. The changes are a continuation and development of the existing and expected results of IT solutions obliging municipalities to obtain and verify themselves certain of the persons applying for these benefits. These changes will be applied for the first time in determining the right to benefits for the period beginning on 1 October 2017. Here I would like to point out that teaching could have been done online. The ease of applying from abroad creates opportunities that were not previously available if applications were not submitted online and were only made in paper form. Therefore, there is a proposal to equip local authorities with this possibility. What are the other changes? It is the alternate care, the coordination of the social security system in voivodships and the cancellation of the rise in income criteria and the early submission of applications. In addition, this proposal clarifies certain provisions of the Law on State aid for the raising of children, including those concerning the establishment of the right to provide education to children under the care of alternate parents of non-family parents, and harmonises and clarifies the provisions of the Law on family benefits and the Law on assistance to persons entitled to maintenance. What else, what other changes does the project envisage? We propose to transfer from January of the new year 2018 from local governments of voivodships to the competence of voivodships of family benefits, maintenance fund benefits and educational benefits implemented in connection with the coordination of social security systems. I would like to point out here that this task has always been carried out as a task commissioned by the government, i.e. this is a task that the government can carry out itself or may commission e.g. Local governments. Cancellation of the increase in income criteria for family benefits planned from 1 November 2017 and the increase in family benefits. Another change, which is important for beneficiaries, for families, important - speeding up the deadline for applying for education. Since 2018, applications for parental benefits, family benefits and benefits from the maintenance fund for new allowance periods can be submitted by electronic means as early as 1 July, but this is the following year, while at the moment the traditional route from 1 August, as until now, i.e. from 1 August, can be submitted online, traditional, traditional and paper applications can be submitted, and there is a codification of this deadline. From 1 August on the portal of the Ministry of Emp@tia we launch modernised - and in the form of user-friendly wizards - tools for submitting applications online, online, for all types of family benefits, I stress, for all types of family benefits, maintenance benefits and educational provision. This means a complete digitisation of the process of filling in and applying for these benefits. This is very important for families who have many responsibilities, there is no need sometimes to wait in line, but it does not rule out... If anybody wants to do so, she can always do so by a traditional road - go to a social assistance centre or family benefits department and make this most traditional way, often using the help of a prepared worker when submitting an application. The incentive for parents to decide whether to have children is also to provide more childcare facilities under the age of 3. We are saying that our goal is to improve accessibility and reduce the cost of child care. Our solutions will facilitate the setting up and running of childcare institutions under the age of 3 and are designed to reduce the costs of operating facilities. This should involve a reduction in child care fees. This also gives us the opportunity to reconcile, combine the professional responsibilities of parents who raise children. The bill aims to increase the number of places in nursery or children's clubs and to reduce the price of children's stay in institutions, I stress, without any reduction in the quality of care and safety of children who are staying in these nursing places. [[Luckily, you guys pulled out.]] In 2010 there were 511 daycare and daycare units with more than 32 thousand. places of care. Currently, there are around 3.7 thousand. care institutions - crèches, children's clubs, day care providers - which have a total of 100,000. places of care. It is more appropriate to compare this number of places of care than even the number of institutions, because as many of us remember, earlier daycare facilities were large, very numerous, today they are much smaller, more intimate, they create good conditions for childcare. So the right number of places is here, but it is also meaningful that out of this number of crèches operating in 2010 we reached the number of 3.7 thousand. an institution of care. As we estimate, in terms of Polish children under 3 years of age covered by this care, the percentage is 11.2% , we are talking about children up to the age of 3 years, in 2010 it was 2.6 percentage points. [[And in 2015?]] But we also have to remember that still, and that's probably surprising for many of us, in more than 70% Polish municipalities lack any institution of care - 70% municipalities. In our strategy, the Moravian Prime Minister's strategy for responsible development assumes that by 2030 there will be care facilities for 33% of children under the age of 3. This can easily be calculated - in this time frame more than 100,000 would have to be created. Places. Deficiencies, especially in these numerous municipalities, make it impossible for many parents to reconcile family responsibilities with working life, and sometimes this is an obstacle, an important obstacle to deciding that a new member, a new child, will appear in the family. What expenses do we propose for this purpose? In order to improve this situation, it is proposed, first of all, to increase the expenditure of the state budget to support the development of crèches, children's clubs and day care institutions from the current PLN 151 million to PLN 500 million. This amount of 0.5 billion - 250 million will come from the state budget and the other 250 million from the Labour Fund. Of these measures, I am talking here about the 250 million from the Labour Fund, it would be financed by a programme to support the professional activation of persons who are forced to give up their jobs due to the need to take care of their children under 3 years of age and because of the lack of care institutions they could benefit from. Support from this source of funding, I am talking about the Labour Fund, would be addressed to municipalities in those districts where high unemployment is present, e.g. unemployment at 150% the national average, in order to activate women with children and to increase the number of childcare facilities in the territory concerned. These measures would therefore promote direct professional activation. We must bear in mind that one of the reasons why young parents are inactive is sometimes the need to take care of the youngest children due to the insufficient number of places in care institutions. And it should also be made clear here that in a situation where, for example, there is no self-government care centre, there is only a day care facility run by a private body, payment for the stay of the child is so high that it is an important obstacle for parents to use these places. We also need to remember that since 70% The municipalities do not have any of these institutions, this also applies to municipalities with high unemployment, areas where earnings are significantly lower than in large agglomerations. We estimate that within a few years, thanks to these measures, because these measures are not a one-year programme, there will be a lot of places to be created, which will also reduce the fees that parents bear so far. In our view, these solutions will contribute to ensuring the high territorial accessibility of these institutions, and will enable parents to choose the form of care they provide for the youngest children - we are talking about choosing whether it will be institutional care, whether it will be a nursery, a children's club, whether it will be a day care provider - or the form of such non-institutional care, we are talking about nannies. What do we want to make it easier to create new places of care? These include the abolition of the requirement to have at least two premises in a nursery intended for the stay of the child, the subsequent exemption of nursery and children's clubs from charges on property owned by the State Treasury or local government units and the exemption of nursery and children's clubs from property tax, the extension of the catalogue of entities capable of establishing nursery and children's clubs and the hire of daily carers. We are talking about individuals other than municipalities and also public institutions, so he will be able to e.g. voivodship local government will also be able to powiats, so if it is not local units. It is proposed that children can stay in children's clubs for up to 10 hours as in daycare, but clubs will be smaller and more intimate. There will be up to 30 children, no more than 30 children. Safety first. In order to increase the safety of children in nursery and children's clubs, it is proposed to introduce nutritional standards, the obligation for carers and volunteers to provide regular first-aid training, as well as the possibility for parents to appoint a parent's council, which will be able to inspect the nursery rooms and the documentation on meals given to children, and to present and consult proposals for activities in these institutions. In the draft law, we propose that these new provisions, which I mentioned here regarding this care, should apply from 1 January 2018. We also want to make the Large Family Card more attractive, increase the right group and improve the availability of the card. Thus, the project also includes amendments to the Large Family Charter Act granting families a number of rights, discounts: statutory discounts and commercial discounts. The changes are intended to make the Large Family Charter more attractive and adapt it to the latest digital technologies. In practice, this will mean introducing a large family card document in the form of applications on mobile devices, tablets, smartphones. It is this application that will allow you to use the card as soon as it has been granted by the governor, mayor or president of the city without having to wait for the printout of this plastic medium, which has been in force so far, is in force and will be in force. The parents will have all the children's cards on their phone. The new regulations would apply from 1 January 2018. This is not the end of our proposal to change the Big Family Charter. At the beginning of the following year, i.e. not 2018 but 1 January 2019, the provisions granting the rights of the Large Family Charter would enter into force also to those parents who ever had three children to depend on. The principle of equal treatment by public authorities would therefore be implemented. I am addressing the High House, my fellow Members, because I am also a parliamentarians, including the opposition, asking for a good, substantive debate on the bill. The family is an extraordinary value and I am glad that these proposals are intended to significantly improve the support that is addressed to families. The Prime Minister often stresses that family policy is an investment. We invest in people. We're investing in people because we're building a strong country. This really is an investment worth the money that is spent on it. I would like to ask you to have a careful, calm, substantive discussion. And I hope that, given the well-being of Polish families, which - I believe deeply - lies at the heart of everyone present and even those who will come to the vote... I hope that you will support the package of changes we have proposed, which we have worked very carefully and carefully on. I ask the High House and all parliamentary clubs to support our proposals. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The bill submitted, as stated in the explanatory memorandum, is another point in the development of effective and effective family policy, the main objective of which is to encourage Polish families to have more children, to remove barriers to their possession, and to introduce further facilitations in their upbringing. The draft law is the implementation of the proposals and recommendations contained in the review of family support schemes, where there are proposals for changes in the area of both education and family benefits, the collection of child support, care for children up to the age of 3 and the Charter of the Large Family. In these areas, the government proposes to introduce changes that will eliminate undesirable effects observed during the first year of implementation of the And so it should be mentioned that the main principles of the 500+ family scheme will remain unchanged. It will continue to be an untaxed benefit of 500 PLN, paid up to 18 years of age per child, with income criterion 800 and 1200 PLN net per person in the family respectively, for one child, of course. The proposed changes in the area of family benefits, of course, concern, among others.: the exclusion of the use of the income loss mechanism when the person concerned loses employment or other employment and will be rehired with the same employer within 3 months or starts a non-agricultural business, changes in the way in which the income of non-agricultural entrepreneurs is determined, demands from persons claiming to raise their children alone with child support from the second parent or the right to provide education to children under the care of alternate parents. The second area or second level of change concerns what will change in family benefits and the maintenance fund. Since 1 November 2017, the government proposes to cancel the increase in income criteria for family benefits and to increase family benefits. The government also proposes that from 1 August 2017 it be possible to handle all requests for 500+ and family benefits online. The next area is the proposed changes in daycare. Their aim is to increase the number of places in crèches or children's clubs and lower prices for children's stay in these institutions without any deterioration of the quality of care provided. This is to be served, among others. increase of expenditure from the current PLN 151 million to PLN 500 million or e.g. exemption of crèches and children's clubs from property charges, which are owned by the State Treasury or local government. The next area of change concerns the Charter of the Large Family, which has already been mentioned by the Minister. The change consists in introducing an electronic Large Family Card without having to wait for a printout of a plastic card. Most importantly, it is proposed that the rights of the Large Family Charter should also be extended to parents who have ever had at least three children. This will be in the opinion of the government, but also in our view, the implementation of the principles of equal treatment by public authorities. Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! In our view, these solutions are very good, expected and desirable solutions. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I request that this proposal be directed to further work in the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u151	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending certain laws related to family support schemes. The bill submitted is another step in the development of Polish family policy. I am glad that the government continues its pro-family policy in 2016. The draft government law introduces numerous facilitations for families with children. Projectors, among others. wish to extend the list of entities that could set up nursery or children's clubs and abolish the requirement to have two separate rooms: a living room and a bedroom. In my opinion, the introduction of these changes will facilitate the establishment and operation of these facilities. It will also reduce the costs of the hosts, which in turn will reduce the costs incurred by parents sending the child to a nursery or a children's club. The changes introduced are intended to increase safety in childcare institutions up to 3 years old. Among other things, it is required to check new employees for sexual offences in the public register. In my opinion, this is a good solution that will ensure the safety of children and calm parents who send their children to institutions that care for the youngest. Large ease and reduction of costs will result in the possibility of running nursery or children's clubs in one preschool place. Such a solution will increase the availability of these institutions for parents with pre-school and nursery children. In my opinion, the introduction of food standards developed by the Food and Nutrition Institute will improve the quality of meals administered. In addition, the creation of parental advice, which will serve as a consultative role, will affect the choice of wholesome meals. I also consider it appropriate to supplement the current list of qualifications of staff employed in crèches or children's clubs with additional courses of study and to agree to work with children in crèches or children's club for persons who have a higher education in any direction of study whose thematic scope covers issues related to the care or development of a young child. An important issue in my opinion is the introduction of additional requirements for staff, among others. concerning 80 hours of supplementary training and participation in first aid training every 2 years. In my opinion, additional requirements will contribute to the safety of children. The purpose of the bill on the Charter of the Large Family was to introduce solutions that would support families of many children and at the same time build their positive image. The Large Family Card gives the entitled a lot more privileges. Children in card families are provided, among others. cheaper access to recreation, which in my opinion will positively affect their development. The rights to the card are also to be obtained by parents who have raised three children, regardless of the fact that children are now adults. In my opinion, this is the right solution because of the equal treatment of citizens by power. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceeding on this bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u153	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Joint Committees: the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Infrastructure on the Government's draft amendment to the Act on certain forms of housing promotion and certain other laws. The joint committees: the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Infrastructure after first reading and considering the draft law at its meeting on 6 June invite the Sejm to adopt the draft law. Ladies and gentlemen, as Poland, we are still facing a great challenge related to housing. In the European Union, we take 24th place in terms of residential space per person: 27.6 m2 in Poland with an average in the European Union of 40 m2. Every tenth Pole dreams of having a bathroom, one in five apartments in Poland does not have heating, and every thirty Polish family does not even have running water. At the same time Poland has as many as 83% proprietary housing resources, while e.g. in rich Germany, more than half of the population of the country lives in rent apartments. In 2015, the average housing resources in the European Union were 486 apartments per 1000 citizens, and in Poland only 363 apartments per 1000 inhabitants. We therefore lack about 1/4 of the number of apartments for the European average. At the same time, it should be stressed that the years 2016 and 2017 represent the next years of growth in the sales of apartments on the primary market. In spite of this, the housing deficit in Poland reaches 7 and 8 million premises, and more than half of Poles have no chance of borrowing and buying their own apartment. Those who have taken credit often occupy it with great effort, including many generations of the entire family. One might wonder, putting aside problems concerning foreign exchange loans, what would happen today in Poland if interest rates increased and returned even to levels from 2012, i.e. to levels before interest rates were reduced. According to analysts, anyone who's borrowed 100,000 in the last period. PLN for 30 years, will pay today a instalment of about PLN 505 per month. Every 100,000. PLN is PLN 505 per month for 30 years. Returning to the 2012 interest rates, counting this into today's instalments, is PLN 715 per month for every 100,000. zł borrowed in the bank. This is an increase of 42%, which poses a huge risk to domestic budgets. So can Poland afford to close the housing deficit and build the missing housing estates for Poles? Let's try to count it. Assuming the price for 1 m2 of the apartment does not exceed 3 thousand. PLN, the cost of the standard 2-room apartment is approx. 200,000. zł. Assuming the market meets the needs of between 120 and 140 thousand. apartments per year, assuming that the need is over 200,000. Apartments per year, we would have to spend up to 12 billion PLN per year from the state budget. The total cost of closing the housing deficit is probably over PLN 400 billion from the budget. Of course, such money will never be able to afford the budget, and therefore the National Housing Programme provides for different building support instruments for different groups of customers. One of them is launched on 1st November 2015 the housing promotion programme in the field of social rental construction. The scheme was implemented under the Law of 26 October 1995 on certain forms of housing promotion, as amended by the Act of 10 September 2015 amending the Act on certain forms of housing promotion and certain other laws which entered into force on 25 October 2015. The financial preferences granted by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego were directed to qualified investors: social housing companies, housing associations and municipal companies, carrying out projects aimed at building apartments for medium-income households, in particular those who raise at least one child. According to the programme, over 10 years BGK will provide PLN 4.5 billion for the programme, while the state budget will be less than PLN 752 million. This funding is intended to ensure that at least 30 000 are created. apartments for rent with rent to medium-income people. According to the Act, the condition for participation in the programme is the conclusion of an agreement between the investor and the municipality, within the framework of the local housing policy of the local government. The draft amendment to the Act presented to the High House is the result of an analysis carried out after the first edition of the submission of applications, including on the basis of information provided by social investors in the rental building and Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. Designed solutions are designed to facilitate investors' implementation of their projects, including in the form of cooperative housing. Currently, it is possible to apply for financial support only for the construction of apartments for rent. The proposed changes allow the financing of other usable areas which will serve to meet needs, e.g. older people. The obligation to enter into an agreement between the borrower and the municipality was waived, indicating a minimum number of dwellings, the tenants of which will be persons raising at least one child, at a level of not less than 50%. all the locations. This was to a large extent a barrier to participation in the programme in the case of local communities with lower participation of families with children. Preferences for the raising of children will, of course, remain strengthened by the point criterion set out in the Regulation. In order to facilitate the financing of projects implemented under the social housing programme, the maximum amount of the tenant's participation in the building costs has been increased from 25% to 30%. This will provide greater flexibility required at the funding stage and will also affect the relatively lower rents in the resources of social housing companies. The formula for calculating the amount of participation reimbursed in the construction costs of the dwelling was also clarified. The project also increased the income limits for renting a dwelling where the construction of the premises was linked to the conclusion of a participation agreement providing for participation in the costs of an investment and construction project at a level of not less than 10%. The reason for the amendment is that the amendment of the Act of 10 September 2015 lowering the level of income limits could in some cases cause difficulties in enforcing from the tenant claims arising from the use of the apartment in the event of a loss of employment by one of the household members. An important change for the tenant is the adoption of more flexible rules when the tenant exceeds the income thresholds set out in the Act. Resigned, among others. the obligation to automatically increase the rent rate in the case of a small even surplus of household income. I would like to ask the High House to refer the draft amendment to the law for further examination in the joint committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The draft law also contains provisions to extend the catalogue of financial instruments for crediting construction. I am surprised that we are so late in making use of financial instruments such as bonds, which, after all, are the natural basis for housing investments worldwide. The project also introduces a change in the area of participation. Among other things, it seals the area of assignment and increases the maximum level of participation to 30%. The solution that provokes additional questions is certainly an increase in the income thresholds for tenants who report a minimum of 10% wish to participate. This may lead to a situation where such a minimum contribution becomes a standard. This will undoubtedly bring greater peace in TBS and other organisations using this type of funding. But the question is, is it the right road in social construction for sure? The Act also introduces new rules for the replacement of premises under TBS and flexible rules for the change of rent. Such a mix of obstacles can cause young families, with young children, to have no chance of living under a feedback programme. Another controversial proposal is to introduce the possibility of financing additional usable areas for older people only. Of course, seniors need our support, and there is nothing to discuss with them, but really, Minister, I do not understand the lack of a similar way of financing the area needed for those who raise children or even children. After all, such families, and children in particular, also need certain additional areas for proper development. For us, this is incomprehensible discrimination against such families, those who raise children. Therefore, on behalf of the Poselski Club Modern make two amendments. The first one maintains preferences for people raising at least one child, but we propose reducing this preference to the volume of premises from 50 to 30%, i.e. 30% in this building was intended for families, children's parents. On the other hand, the second amendment [[♪ The bell ♪]] introduces the possibility of financing additional useful areas for the needs of children. On behalf of the Modern Posel Club, I ask the High House to accept these amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The Joint Sejm Committees on 7 June 2017 proposed to amend the wording of the current title of the Act, correcting it by replacing the existing part by the expression "organisational units', support units of municipalities, buildings, objects and public utility facilities and monuments. Such a change seems rational, for example, because as a result of the amendment of the law, the organisational units and auxiliary units of the municipalities will be burdened with new tasks and obligations arising directly from the disposal of the matter under review today. Thus, Chapter 1 of the amended law rightly defines what is considered to be ancillary units of the municipalities. This chapter deals with the procedure of dealing with a case which is particularly complicated in the situation of service of the voivodship's request. The mere expectation of an opinion shall suspend the period for annulment of the act. Chapter 2 already in the wording of the report of the committee in an enumerative manner against the prohibition of the promotion of communism or other totalitarian system by monuments clarifies what or who these monuments previously erected cannot relate to. Enumeratively, constructions shall be replaced, by which the word "memory" must be literally understood. For a reminder: they can be mounds, obelisks, statues, busts, plates and plaques, sculptures or souvenir stones. This provision makes appropriate exemptions on the application of the matter of the law. It is worth recalling that the legislator does not provide for the application of the provisions to monuments located in cemeteries or other resting places, entered in the register of monuments which are not public and artistic or scientific, provided that they do not promote the totalitarian system. The recipe defines quite broad voivodship cultivation ordering both the owner and the user of perpetual property to remove a monument promoting communism or other totalitarian system. The relevant tasks are imposed on the Institute of National Remembrance - the Commission for the Investigation of Crimes against the Polish Nationals, which follows from Chapters 2 and 3. The Commission also decided on the costs of removing the monument, which will be charged to the owner or the perpetual user of the property. The legislator imposes a special obligation on the local government units, the association of these units and the metropolitan union to remove all the symbols referred to in the amended law within 12 months of the date of entry into force of the law. However, if the owner or user of the perpetual property on which the monument subject to the removal under this Act was located did not participate in the procedure for the erection of this monument, the costs of its removal shall be reimbursed by the Treasury from the State budget. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will vote in favour of the report of the joint committees, contained in print no. 1616. The local authorities, who had not made such a change so far, probably thought that this person deserved some commemoration. This law does not allow any possibility for these local authorities to in a sense appeal and show that such changes should not be made. Why do you not allow self-governments to vote, who sometimes know better who was in history? Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u209	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the club Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Act on Local Workers in Printing No.1324, which assumes the removal of the provisions on political offices operating at the various levels of local government, at the entrance, mayor, city president, seniors or Marshal of the voivodeship. The project assumes that contracts with local government staff of political offices employed as advisers, assistants will be terminated as of the date of entry into force of the above law. In the current legal system, individual heads of government, mayors, presidents, seniors and marshals have the possibility of hiring assistants and advisers on the basis of a contract of employment. On the other hand, the Act does not specify the tasks to be performed by these persons in these positions as advisors or assistants. It is also worth noting that the remuneration rules for advisers and assistants are the same as for other local staff. The advisers and assistants shall be entitled to the remuneration appropriate to the position occupied and the professional qualifications held, including the basic salary, the allowance for the long-term work, the Jubilee Prize and all the associated privileges. The liquidation of political offices in local governments will reduce red tape and look for savings in the administration and thus respond to social expectations. The Law and Justice Club supports and will work on this project during the committee meeting. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u231	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni wheels, I present my position towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Local Workers Act. The projectors stress that the changes introduced by the Act meet social expectations, as it will save nearly PLN 50 million. The very idea of changing designers is good, but I believe that there is no accurate and complete data in this area. Not all voivodships were able to obtain the necessary data, which in my opinion would allow me to assess the number of people employed as assistants or advisors. I must also stress for the second time that not all of us have the right knowledge in every area. Therefore, in my opinion, hiring assistants and advisers at the level of self-government and state administration is essential for the smooth functioning of many offices of different levels. They should not only be perceived by the economic prism. Often their work contributes to the successful implementation of projects in various fields. The arguments put forward by the designer are not convincing to me. Projectors claim that assisting and advisory positions are often linked personally to persons in a given local government unit with managerial functions. It's hard to imagine an employee not getting involved in the success of his employer. The basis for fruitful cooperation is mutual trust. The termination of the employment relationship on the date of entry into force of the procedated amendments to the Local Staff Act is incompatible with Article. 30 Constitution of the Republic of Poland and Art. Sixty-three, sixty-sixth of the Labour Code. It lacks the task of assistants and advisors. They are appointed by the heads of office, but this does not mean full freedom to shape responsibilities. Of course, it is important to eliminate the illegal employment element without any competitions. During the committee's work, it has been stressed on several occasions that those who are currently in assistant and advisory positions will start to move to other positions where they will be fully employed. This situation, in my opinion, will start to generate a constant and at the same time higher cost of living for local government employees. The committee claimed that the employment of an assistant or advisor was linked to the continuation of a specific project. Not every local government unit has assistants or advisers. Each authority shall decide on a case-by-case basis. The Act contains a provision that regulates employment without a competition of assistants and advisors depending on the number of inhabitants of a given local government unit. I do not understand the reservations about the work of assistants who were previously involved in political work. Thanks to their experience, the employer is guaranteed not to be a random person but to have a wide range of knowledge in various fields. I agree with the decisions of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, and in view of the numerous doubts raised by the draft law, we will vote against it.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u234	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. The first vote will be taken by Bożena Borys-Raccoon, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u235	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the government draft law amending the Employment Promotion Act and the Labour Market Institutions Act and some other laws, print No 1494. This law is the implementation in Polish law of Directive 2014/36/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 February 2014 on the conditions of entry and residence of third-country nationals for employment as a seasonal worker. In addition, the amendments aim to seal the procedure for short-term work by the citizens of six countries, i.e. Armenia, Belarus, Georgia, Moldova, the Russian Federation or Ukraine, on the basis of a statement by the delegate at the county labour office. The Directive introduced requires Member States to introduce conditions and procedures for granting residence permits and seasonal work for foreigners. The Act also aims to reduce the identified fraud related to the system of declarations. In addition, it introduces changes of order and sealing nature in the issue of work permits for foreigners. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, in support of the project, requests that it be passed on to further proceding. At the same time, I submit 10 amendments to the project on behalf of the club. Nine of them concern the Employment Promotion Act and the Employment Market Institutions, and one concerns the Foreigners Act. Most amendments aim to make the rules more flexible and reduce administrative burdens, especially for farmers and fruit farmers, as they concern a new seasonal work permit. The proposed changes will allow: quicker assignment of work to a foreigner, immediately on the day of transfer to the labour office of the required documents after the entry of the foreigner to Poland, and in the case of holiday days or days free of work of the office immediately after the entry of the foreigner to Poland - provided that the required documents are transferred to the labour office on the first day of the office's work, the amount of information contained in the application is reduced to the minimum necessary and that the person entrusting the work to the foreigner has the funds to pay the seasonal workers, by deleting as grounds for repealing the seasonal work permit. One of the amendments consists in reducing the lower limit of penalties for entrusting the foreigner with illegal work to a level of PLN 1000, as in the Labour Code for offences against the rights of the worker. On the other hand, for the sneaky introduction to the illegal assignment of work to a foreigner and for the illegal execution of work by a foreigner should be maintained to the level of 3000 PLN. One amendment obliges the Minister for Labour to publish in the Public Information Bulletin every month information on the state of use of work permits or declarations, provided that the limits are introduced, which will facilitate the recruitment of employers in good time. The other amendments are specific or aim to ensure consistency with current or proposed solutions. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Reducing bureaucratic employment procedures to carry out seasonal work enabling fruit and vegetable producers to collect agricultural fruits should be a priority, as this determines our competitiveness in European and global markets. Unfavorable rules are very easy to bring to an end the fruit and vegetables sector, but it is much harder to return and rebuild production, as we all know very well, because similar things happened to other branches of the Polish industry. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u267	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much for the discussion, for supporting the draft amendment in general, but also for the questions. I would like to answer these questions in two categories. One question is typically the seasonal directive that affects farmers, gardeners and the tourism industry. Of course, one of the demands is not to implement the seasonal directive. Let me remind you that we have an implementation procedure concerning the non-implementation of the directive, the deadline for implementing this directive was 30 September. r. It is possible to adopt such a principle that we do not implement any directive and pay penalties for it. I don't think it would be worth it for you as a whole. Indeed, the seasonal directive concerns these three sectors and requires authorisation. The second area of activity, which has been conducted in Poland for many years, is the so-called area. permits for short-term work on the basis of statements in the case of six third countries, so they are called, of course, citizens of Ukraine. Now we have to settle. This relationship, when the seasonal directive was not implemented many years ago, mainly concerned agriculture. It's not like that today. Today agriculture is still a leading group that benefits from this simplified system, but today the whole economy, including construction, trade, services, benefits from the latter. 70% claims already apply to industries other than agriculture. That's what we're dealing with, what we have to deal with. As far as agriculture is concerned, the talks we held in both the agricultural committee and farmers, particularly those producing the so-called ones. Soft fruits are designed to adopt such a principle that this new formula of authorisation is as simple and as easy as possible. We also introduce a number of facilitations, among others. we extend at the request of farmers the period of validity of this authorisation to 9 months. We're introducing the so-called. a multi-season visa for 3 years. After a year, so to speak, legal work will be available for this 3-year visa, so you will no longer need to apply for these visas again. We shorten the deadline for issuing a statement from 3 to 7 days, i.e. the county labour office, where this task remains, within 7 days it must issue a statement of work permit. In more difficult situations this is a 30-day period. We have also introduced, at the request of farmers, a change that, up to 30 days, a third-country national working in agriculture may be entrusted with other work. If there is a change in weather and there is no work in agriculture, within 30 days such a worker may do other work for this farmer and there will also be a permit. We are changing what Mr Arndt asked. The point is that someone is waiting for this request from the voivodeship and has this job as illegal. That's what we're changing. Now, if he re-appends on time, the entire waiting period will be treated as legal. Even if the voivode refuses him and decides that this stay is already an illegal stay, the period during which he worked will be treated as legal, i.e. it will not be treated the other way around. With regard to the transfer of all permits to the gesture of the elders, this discussion, also within the government, was going in the opposite direction - to pass all tasks to the voivodeship offices. We've kept this statement and permission part in the old days because it's easier to get here. I don't really think we're going to go downhill with this - we already have good experiences, even last year, when it was 1300 thousand. statements, county labour offices have dealt with this - all the more so now that these offices will also receive additional funds, as we are introducing a 30-gold payment for the declaration to eliminate part of the so-called. fake statements, made only to obtain visas. Although the situation is also dynamic because, as you know, visa-free travel between Ukraine and European countries has been introduced since 11 June. Of course, there are travel visas, biometric passports, but that also changes the situation somehow. It's a limit issue. These limits, I remind you, are defined in the Act as optional. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u269	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Employment Promotion Act and the Employment Market Institutions and some other laws. The main objective of these amendments is to implement Directive 2014/36/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council. The Directive concerns the conditions of entry and residence of foreigners for employment as a seasonal worker and the sealing of the authorisation procedure for short-term work. The implementation of the directive into Polish legislation introduces significant changes to the law, which expands the current system of admission of foreigners to the Polish labour market. The amended amendment provides for the modification of the current system of registration of work return claims, in which there are currently many abuses. In addition, the Directive introduces seasonal work permits and identifies agriculture, horticulture and tourism as three sectors of the economy in which such work may be of a nature. Importantly, all third-country nationals will be able to apply for this type of work. This is undoubtedly a significant extension of the current employment opportunities of foreigners on our market. Among the proposed provisions are also a number of such ones that guarantee the safety and decent conditions of employment for foreigners. The Directive requires that they have the same level of protection, inter alia. to cover them with the appropriate insurance system, which is guaranteed to Polish citizens. In my view, such provisions of the Act will have a positive impact on the reduction of discrimination against workers coming to us from other countries. I also consider it a good solution to introduce optional limits for work permits issued in a given calendar year. When setting limits, account will be taken of the needs of the labour market and the principle of complementarity of employment of foreigners with Polish citizens. I am convinced that this solution will have a positive impact on the reduction of a number of pathological situations, such as. issuing false statements on the assignment of work. The procedure at the county labour offices and consulates has so far been extended due to the registration of such apparent statements. In practice, this has led to the fact that employers actually in need of an employee could not get it quickly. A reduction in the size of these abuses will certainly also serve to introduce a one-off fee for the employer's assignment of work. Among the proposed regulations is also the possibility of creating a labour register for foreigners. In my view, this is a noteworthy solution that will affect safety and at the same time improve the monitoring of the labour market situation. Detailed regulations on work permits for foreigners in Poland are necessary. There is also a need for legal influx of immigrants into Poland, because labour resources in Poland are decreasing from year to year. The legal influx of immigrants is important for the smooth functioning of the economy as well as for the social development of the state. There is no doubt, however, that the legislation under discussion today will be a burden for employers. These solutions will extend the administrative system and procedures and increase expenditure from the state budget. The changes are linked to the creation of 200 new jobs. However, this is a small price incurred in exchange for the benefits of the bill submitted. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding the bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u271	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I submit a report to the Health Commission on the draft law amending the law on anti-drugs, prints 812, 812-A and 1270. The draft draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Drug Addiction aimed at enabling the use of cannabis pharmaceuticals for medical purposes, hereinafter referred to as the draft, entered the Sejm on 1 February 2016. On 5 September 2016, he was assigned to work in the Health Commission. On 20 October 2016, its first reading ended. It was then referred to the Extraordinary Sub-Committee, which met four times, concluding its report on the draft adopted by the Health Commission as submitted, together with the self-correction, on 7 February 2017, print No 1270. The Health Committee's report required an identification procedure which had ended at this stage. In the current draft law of 29 July 2005 on the prevention of drug addiction after the article. 33 the following Article shall be inserted: 33a-d speaking among others the fact that hemp herb other than fibrous and extracts, pharmaceutical tinctures and all other pharmaceutical preparations of hemp other than fibrous and hemp resin other than fibrous hemp referred to in Annex 1 which is a list of narcotic drugs to the Law on the Prevention of Drug Addiction may constitute the pharmaceutical raw material referred to in Article. 2 point 40 of the Pharmaceutical Law Act of 6 September 2001. It's a very important point in the bill. The recognition of cannabis pharmaceutical preparations as pharmaceutical raw materials makes it possible to use them for the preparation of prescription drugs after obtaining a marketing authorisation issued by the President of the Office for the Registration of Medicinal Products, Medical Products and Biocidal Products. To obtain this permission, of course, you must meet the relevant requirements. The conditions to be met by the licence application and other elements which it should contain are set out in the text of the project. There is also a delegation for the Minister of Health to issue a regulation setting out the model for the proposal. In order to clearly define the different stages of the manufacture of pharmaceutical raw materials, it is proposed to divide into two stages. The first stage is the preparation of plant material as a source of the active substance intended for the manufacture of pharmaceutical raw materials. This means grinding dried parts of plants and carrying out physical and chemical operations leading to the extraction and packaging of these substances. The next step is the production of the pharmaceutical raw material itself, which consists in repacking the substance properly from the bulk packaging to the packaging in which the raw material will be delivered to the pharmacies. It is important that the production of pharmaceutical raw materials does not mean cultivation of plants. This is important, it was a point of dispute around which long-term discussions took place. The adoption of the formula that cannabis herb and pharmaceutical preparations made from it can be pharmaceutical raw materials means that it can be used as a means for the production of medicines, which in turn means that it is subject to the criterion of general monograph according to the Polish Pharmacopei and European Pharmacopei and is subject to the supervision of the chief pharmaceutical inspector, so it is ensured that this remedy available in pharmacies will be safe for the patient. The safety of the patient, for whom the measure will be used, which has not yet undergone a complete set of standard clinical trials, is particularly important. At the same time, by recognising hemp herb as a pharmaceutical raw material whose registration is simplified, it can be legally available at a rapid pace. This will allow physicians to use cannabis pharmaceuticals as one of the means to reduce pain and ailments such as nausea or vomiting in cancer patients. These are undoubtedly the advantages of this project... The current shape of this project is an undoubted compromise between the world of science, which approaches the opinion about the healing properties of cannabis herb with reserve, and the world's emotions resulting probably from good intentions, through which the eyes of the imagination can now be seen more than you can see. Thank you very much.

At the moment, you can import medicines made from cannabis herb to Poland, such as Sativex, but the destination import is a troublesome and time-consuming procedure, so the adoption of a law in the current form allows you to obtain a medicine prepared in an authorised pharmacy much faster, probably even in one day. The safety of the patient will also be monitored by the Chief Sanitary Inspector. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u313	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I was honoured to present on behalf of the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism a report by the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism on the Government's draft law amending the Sports Act and the Act on Disclosure of Documents of State Security Authorities from 1944 to 1990 and the content of these documents, print No 1410. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism for first reading on 22 March 2017. The Commission, following its first reading and the examination of this draft law at its meetings on 20 April, 11 May and 7 June 2017, invites the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. Ladies and gentlemen! The Sports Act in force since 2010 had to be amended as soon as possible. Such a need has already been pointed out by a rather short period of validity of the law, which significantly strengthened the political position of Polish sports unions. This privilege was not fully read, in a large part in the authorities of Polish sports unions, which quite carelessly approached, for example, the alignment of their statutes with the requirements of the current law. This can be demonstrated by the number of decisions issued by the Minister of Sport, whether positive or negative, approving statutory changes in Polish sports unions at the end of 2012. It should be remembered that this position of Polish sports unions was further strengthened in 2013 by the new concept of financing sport pushed by the then Minister of Sport and Tourism, according to which the concept of Polish sports unions was to be based on the central financing of professional sport in our country. The said Sports Act of 2010 established, in relation to Polish sports unions, orders and prohibitions to force them to introduce corporate governance rules, e.g. ban on the annual audit of financial statements by the statutory auditor, ban on the conduct by members of the boards of Polish sports unions of business activities on the subject of the activity of Polish sports unions. However, these records proved to be a fiction, as can be seen from other scandals and scandals, such as those in the Polish Association of Light Athletics - the case of javelin, walkers or corruption in the program of building athletic stadiums, the scandal in the Polish Kolar Union with the construction of the track in Pruszkow, the acrobatic scandal, when a 16-year-old player of the Polish Gymnastic Society The fact that it is bad in Polish sport proved in 2015. The Supreme Audit Chamber, which audited the way the Ministry of Sport and Tourism subsidised preparations for the London Olympic Games in 2012 through Polish sports unions. Most of the Polish sports unions, which prepared our staff for the Olympic Games in London, wrongly spent public money for this purpose, which undoubtedly had a negative impact on the results of sports players and affected Poland's poor medal position on the Olympic Games. I think that the titles of the press articles on this subject speak for themselves: half a billion zlotys drowned. NIK took a look at Polish sports, Polish sports unions ate half a billion! There's a suspicion of corruption! As it turned out, money was only meant for athletes, and it wasn't. The lack of control of Polish sports unions, the cessation of monitoring of the implementation of sport development strategy in Poland until 2015, the lack of a vision of changes and repair of Polish sport by the subsequent management of the Ministry of Sport of the previous coalition led to further adverse developments in Polish sports unions. Such a situation could lead to further preparations of players to compete in competitions, especially the Olympic Games in Rio. These and other situations, as well as numerous voices from the sports community, from the players themselves, trainers, trade union activists, supporters, have determined to take corrective action and the changes presented to the High House in this draft Act amending the Sports Act and the Act on disclosure of State Security Authorities documents from 1944 to 1990 and the content of these documents, print No 1410. Ladies and gentlemen! This is another important step - courageous and important - made by the Ministry of Sport and Tourism in changing the face of Polish sport in an institutional dimension, building a positive image of Polish sports unions, fighting pathology, nepotism, promoting ethical behaviour, excluding corruption and finally transparency and transparency of public spending. The aim of the draft amendment of the law is to increase transparency of the operation of Polish sports unions. The exclusion of the phenomenon of corruption, the already mentioned nepotism in sport, requires a limitation of capital, organizational and personal links with the members of the trade union bodies. Therefore, one of the most important solutions is the prohibition of capital links for board members and their families. Transparency of the activities of Polish sports unions will also increase the ban on the role of a member of the management board of the Polish sports union by former officers of the security authorities of the State of the Polish People's Republic. Those involved in the functioning of Polish sports unions should enjoy a good reputation so that they cannot be accused of behaviour contrary to moral, ethical and legal standards. The project also provides for increased supervision by the Minister of Sport and Tourism of Polish sports unions by examining the compatibility of their activities with the internal regulations of the association, e.g. Rules or instructions. The new solutions will allow to supervise the functioning of Polish sports union authorities, such as the board of directors or internal control bodies, to a much wider extent than before. We're talking most of the time about the review committee. According to the project, the Minister of Sport and Tourism will be able to award periodic sports scholarships to players who, for reasons independent of themselves, for example. injury, uncontrolled fall, exclusion from competition, did not obtain the result of sports entitlement to benefits. This will allow athletes who are in a particular situation to continue preparing for the event of the championship rank indicated in the Act. This, ladies and gentlemen, is also one of the many long-awaited and long-awaited changes that have been included in the draft law by athletes. Then, by symbolically increasing the scope of the concept of sport and including activities considered to be sports also competition based on intellectual activity, the aim of which is to achieve the outcome of sport, we enable in disciplines such as chess, checkers or sports bridge the impact of programs promoting mobility in these sports environments and in an evolving e-sport environment. Here, ladies and gentlemen, it is important to remember that the new law absolutely does not align traditional sport with the new sport, e-sport, which is also developing more and more in our country. Increasing transparency of Polish sports unions and preventing conflicts of interest is intended to serve a number of additional restrictions. According to the draft amendment, a member of the management board of the Polish sports association will not be able to combine this function e.g. with work at the Ministry of Sport and Tourism. Nor will he be able to train national staff or to serve as a training staff in the same sport. The rules on registration of sports clubs in the register of old age, sports scholarships, prizes for outstanding sports achievements and Olympic benefits, as well as sports clubs forming part of the professional league are also clarified. In the course of the legislative work so far, amendments of the editorial, ordering nature and the substantive amendment which I tabled at the committee meeting were tabled. This amendment concerned the introduction of a ban on the management of the Polish sports union for persons punished with disciplinary penalties for doping in sport in a unit dimension of more than 24 months. This amendment was accepted. High Izbo! Procededed changes confirm the introduction of another good quality change in Polish sport. After the act on combating doping in sport we stand the chance to cleanse Polish sport, especially with certain phenomena of pathology, corruption or nepotism. The new sports law will influence the construction of a positive image of sports organisations, including activists, trainers and players, promotion of ethical behaviour, transparency and transparency in the spending of public money transferred to Polish sports unions by the Minister of Sports and Tourism, and thus hopefully to achieve better and better sporting results by our representatives. At this point, I would like to thank you in particular for your personal commitment and for the presence in the legislative work here in the Sejm of the Minister for Sport and Tourism, Mr Witold Banuka, his colleagues, headed by the Secretary of State, Mr Jarosław Stawiarski, and the Directorates of the Legal Office of the Ministry of Sport. As at the outset, I ask the High House to pass the attached bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u325	Mrs. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! This draft law deals with a number of issues which can be broken down into two groups. The first is to add a number of prohibitions applicable to the performance of the function of a member of the board of the Polish sports union, and the second is to extend the definition of sport to competition based on intellectual activity, to extend scholarship programmes for outstanding young people, to regulate the powers of the minister, etc. The current regulation and definition of sport focuses solely on sport as physical activity. The project therefore extends the concept of sport to intellectual activity, allowing for the inclusion of sport types such as: chess, checkers or sports bridge. It is also a canvas to deal with the issue of e-sports in the future. And here's a small request, bow to the Minister. Because at the committee meeting, the Minister himself mentioned that e.g. In France it is regulated by the Ministry of Digitalization, I will take steps to ensure that the Ministry of Digitalization takes care of the e-sport. On the other hand, I hope that the Minister as well as his colleagues They're gonna help us sort it out a little bit. Importantly, the scholarship programme for outstanding young people is also expanding, introducing a certain amount of flexibility. If an outstanding young athlete is unlucky and gets injured just before important competitions, it will no longer be equivalent to the end of support from the state, which often means the end of a career for a promising player. The rest of the Act refers to the functioning of members of the Polish sports union. These are mainly provisions to prevent corruption and conflicts of interest. The Act introduces e.g. prohibition of holding shares and shares in companies working for the union and similar exclusion from members of the immediate family of a person operating in the PZS. Very good changes. We can only ask ourselves how it is possible that such situations could have taken place so far. The Act also provides that former officials of the security authorities on the boards and control bodies of Polish sports unions lose their functions within 6 months of its entry into force. And it will allow you to see if what my previous speaker said is right to be there, whether they are actually no longer there, or whether someone will lose that function. A separate issue is whether an agent's past is to disqualify a very good sports manager. Apart from this, in conclusion, the law regulates a number of issues and it could easily be divided into two or even three projects. We could then consider each regulation individually. In this case, we have to decide on this whole package, but despite some objections, Kukiz15 supports the proposed project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u328	Thank you very much. Please, Mr Mieczysław Kazimierz Baszko, Polish People's Party.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u329	Mrs. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party to present a position towards the government's draft law on the amendment of the Sports Act and the Act on disclosure of documents of state security authorities from 1944 to 1990 and the content of these documents, prints Nos 1410 and 1600. The project deals with increasing transparency of the operation of Polish sports unions and preventing conflicts of interest by introducing further restrictions on membership of former officers of the security authorities of the State of the Polish People's Republic on the Management Board of the Polish Sports Association, improving transparency of the operation of Polish sports unions, extending the definition of sport to intellectual activity, the aim of which is to achieve the result of sports, introducing the possibility of awarding a sports scholarship in particularly justified cases. As regards former officers of the security authorities of the State of the Polish People's Republic, as well as the possibility of awarding a sports scholarship in particularly justified cases, the Polish People's Party does not comment. However, I would like to make comments on the above. projects in the field of Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 point 6, which in our opinion are extremely important to ensure the smooth functioning of each Polish sports union, in particular as regards the possibility of raising funds for statutory activities. In a situation of constant reduction of expenditure on public tasks in the field of sport carried out by Polish sports unions, there is a need to look for other non-budget sources of financing for the activities of Polish sports unions. One way to generate funds is to create dedicated commercial law companies, whose task is to support the Polish sports union. The legislator, of course, allows the creation of commercial companies by Polish sports unions, but recently proposed amendments to the Sports Act on the wording of the article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 point 6 indicates that the intention is to prevent members of the management board of the Polish sports association from acting as a member of the body, proxy or representative in entities engaged in economic activities related to the performance of its statutory tasks by the association. From the above justification it is not possible to agree, for example, because it is difficult to imagine a conflict of interest in a situation where the Polish sports union is established by a company in which it is the only shareholder, and the purpose of this company is the organizational and financial support of a given Polish sports union. The restrictions imposed by the proposed rules will make Polish sports unions lose influence on the day-to-day functioning and direct control of entities whose associations can and generally are owners. It is difficult to find a reasonable justification for the establishment of regulations that would not have the right to appoint such bodies, e.g. management boards and supervisory boards, persons who are also members of the management board of the Polish sports union. If the union is established by the company, it is natural to want to exercise ongoing supervision of the company's activities. The restrictions proposed by the legislator differ substantially from the general standards laid down in particular by the Commercial Companies Code for all other participants in legal transactions. Certainly, the proposed restriction to the board members of the Polish sports association should not apply to a situation in which the Polish sports union is the sole shareholder of the commercial law company. It is impossible to see any risks in relations between a subsidiary and a Polish sports association when a member of the management board of the Polish sports association serves as a member of the body of such a company. In conclusion, it should be concluded that the introduction of a restriction on the scope of Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 3 (6) of the Sports Act will adversely affect the functioning of Polish sports unions, which seek alternative sources of financing for sport. Moreover, this regulation also applies directly to all entities engaged in activities related to the implementation by the Polish sports association of its statutory tasks, including sports clubs and even companies created for the management of the professional league. In a situation where all efforts are aimed at raising additional funds, it appears that the proposed regulation significantly limits economic freedom and establishes barriers that are inadequate to the intended objectives. For this reason, we call for clarification of the provision of the Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 point 6 of the project by stating that the above prohibition does not apply to companies created by the Polish sport association. We therefore call for this provision to be supplemented by the following wording: except for capital companies whose sole partner is the Polish sport association. In writing, I present and present to your marshal an amendment concerning this point, Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 (6).
