Regarding the most important changes contained in this project, above all this is, as I mentioned earlier, an increase in transparency of the operation of Polish sports unions and the prevention of conflicts of interest by introducing further restrictions concerning members of the board of the Polish sports union; the introduction of a ban on the role of a member of the management of the Polish sports union by former officers of the security bodies of the Polish People's Republic, which makes it possible to specify high ethical and moral requirements for those exercising these functions; the strengthening of the supervision of the Minister of Sport and Tourism over the Polish sports unions, with full autonomy of these sports unions, the possibility of controlling the regulations and internal rules which sports unions themselves establish - it is not so that the Ministry wants to impose some rules whether the internal regulations of Polish sports unions only want to verify how Polish sports unions comply with those regulations which they themselves have established; introduce the possibility for the Minister responsible for physical culture to grant a sports scholarship in particularly justified cases where a member of national staff for reasons independent of himself does not obtain a sporting result which entitles him to receive a sports scholarship; clarify the rules on sports scholarships, prizes for outstanding sports achievements and Olympic benefits, as well as sports clubs belonging to the professional league; increase the scope of the meaningful concept of sport. And this is where I'd like to stay a little bit. The new definition of sport extended to intellectual activity, the aim of which is to achieve a sporting result, includes such sports as chess, checkers or sports bridge. I would also like to make it clear here that the new proposals in no way change the legal status in any way so-called. e-sports, on the other hand, are technical and symbolic changes, are to be an expression of appreciation of individual mental sports - throughout the country, I would like to remind you, there are more than 600 checkers clubs, sports bridge, chess bridge - as well as a symbolic opening to the so-called environment. e-sport. It should be made clear that this amendment does not in any way equal traditional and so-called sport. e-Sport gives us more opportunities to promote and implement programmes promoting physical activity among people practicing so-called. mental sports. The main, overarching task of the Ministry of Sport and Tourism is to promote physical activity. Changes in this law will symbolically increase the possibility of taking actions that physically activate the environment of people dealing with so-called. e-sports and targeting to this large group of social projects raising awareness of the nature of active lifestyles, especially by children and young people. Noticeing the growing interest in the so-called. e-sports, we want to meet this phenomenon and make it clear to young people how important it is to take action within traditional sport for proper development. This is essential. It is not possible to equalize so-called. e-sports with traditional sport. This is absolutely obvious. This change is in some way a symbolic opening to this environment. Ladies and gentlemen, we may be offended that children and young people are playing on the computer, we can forbid it - it's not going to change anything in this area - and we can also draw a hand to this environment, make it clear that you can be more efficient, more efficient in what you do, only and only if you do sports. Today, the best chess players are people who actively play sports. Playing sports also improves the intellectual condition. In this way, absolutely symbolic, we want to open ourselves up to the e-sport environment, completely without equalizing these two different phenomena. This is a very important matter. Moreover, in traditional sports there are Polish sports unions. It is the essence of the difference between traditional sports and so-called. an e-sport. There is no so-called organization. an e-sport that meets the substantive premise of creating a Polish sports association, which is belonging to an international sports federation operating in Olympic or Special Olympic sports or other recognised by the International Olympic Committee. That needs to be made clear. Ladies and gentlemen, it is worth noting that this project has been the subject of extensive consultation with representatives of the sports world. Before submitting the project to the public consultation, the scope of the bill was assessed by the Social Council of Sports, an ancillary body of the Minister of Sport and Tourism composed of outstanding representatives of the sports community, the environment of the world of medicine, science, sport, players, trainers. The project was also submitted for public consultation to all sports unions, the Polish Olympic Committee, the Polish Paralympian Committee and selected sports organisations, with a 21-day deadline for expressing their position, up to 79 entities in total. As a result of these consultations, a number of comments from the sports community have been taken into account in this draft law. As regards the questions of Members of Parliament, I would like to address them briefly. Ladies and gentlemen, I'm a little confounded because I have the impression that some of you have completely failed to read the bill, its records. These questions or positions, in particular Mr Meysztowicz's position, are some kind of publicist and they have nothing to do with the bill. Of course, there is no provision, ladies and gentlemen, in this draft law, which would mirror or require the vetting of players and coaches. Only members of the management board of Polish sports unions will be subject to a illustrative obligation. As regards issues related to the alleged interference of the Ministry of Sport and Tourism in internal regulations and in the autonomy of Polish sports unions, I would like to make it clear to you that there is no possibility of any interference in the autonomy of the functioning of Polish sports unions with regard to both the choice of authorities and internal regulations which Polish sports unions themselves establish. This entry only applies to the fact that we want to check how much Polish sports unions comply with the rules they have established for themselves. This is the only regulation, which is about to increase the transparency of the functioning of sports unions, but in no way will we touch on the question of how Polish sports unions in the field of these internal regulations are to function. I have the impression that a little bit of the state is depreciating the company from O.O., glorifying the legal form of the public limited liability company. Ladies and gentlemen, this is also a bow towards sports clubs: the company z o.o. offers the right opportunities for transparent functioning, and also often the budgets of sports clubs are really very low. The costs of operating public limited liability companies are much higher and, at the same time, we know - as Mr Matuszewski mentioned - that we have a number of cases where the legal formula has not led to the situation that the public limited liability company acted as it should. She asked about the scholarship rate. This is an indicator that already works in the Sports Act. Issues related to doping regulations. Ladies and gentlemen, these anti-doping regulations have been greatly tightened. Today, two years of disqualification may threaten, for example, a contaminated conditioner in the Sports Committee. This is a kind of compromise: you have proposed 18 months, we have prepared, we have proposed 24 months to make it consistent. This comment by Mr Ziółkowski on possible colleagues... Art. The 5th IPN Law explicitly regulates this. We are not, of course, talking about athletes who functioned as regular militia or military staff in sports clubs. This provision naturally does not apply to them. This provision generally concerns members of the boards of Polish sports unions and audit committees. You are talking here about some supposedly huge group of athletes who will be deprived, as you mentioned here, of the possibilities of functioning in society. This does not apply to these provisions of the Act at all. I don't know where the press came from. In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, please accept the bill. It increases the general competence of the monument conservator compared to the voivodship and the voivodship conservators - compared to the heads of individual delegations. This gives a chance for uniform action across the country and will also allow for the harmonisation of the actions of conservationists of voivodship and conservation workers of monuments across the country. It must be remembered that unfortunately Poland is a country in which, as a result of many years of wars, many of the monuments have been destroyed. Those that are still here today are often destroyed in the majesty of the law or on the border of the existing law by new owners. Often the monument conservator was helpless in view of such situations, hence the idea of introducing administrative penalties that could allow a quick reaction to those who would want to destroy such a monument. This is the right thing to do. There is, of course, also the issue of the general supervision of the conservator, which will be the second instance of appeal in the case of administrative penalties, in order to eliminate any irregularities, but also to harmonise the case law and impose possible penalties in the case of unlawful actions. The possibility of imposing administrative penalties will also allow for the creation of a new fund and the collection of additional funds for the care of monuments. The aim is to create a National Monument Protection Fund, to which funds from possible administrative penalties will influence. We hope that soon this fund will be extended, because the funds for the protection of monuments unfortunately still lack in Poland. During the discussion on the bill, we clearly indicated that voivodship conservationists of monuments have too little resources to assist those who want to look after the monuments, because possession of the monument is associated with many limitations. This law gives additional penalties and prohibitions, but there should also be a greater possibility of financial incentives, financial assistance in this difficult work of preserving monuments. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u366	Thank you very much. Ms Anna Wasilewska, Civic Platform, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u367	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club regarding the government draft law amending the Act on the Protection of Monuments and the Care of Monuments and some other laws. The aim of the amendments is to increase the efficiency of the State policy tasks set out in the National Monument Protection and Monument Care Programme adopted by the Council of Ministers Resolution of 24 June 2014. The presented bill enhances the general competence of the monument conservator by enabling him to influence in a decisive way the cast of the posts of the voivodship monument conservators and the heads of the delegations of voivodship monument conservation offices. In addition, it added coordination and control of the activities of the voivodship conservationist of monuments, establishing general directions for their operation, issuing instructions and guidelines on how to proceed and asking the voivodship conservation officer to report on current activities. The general conservator of monuments has individual responsibility for the state of affairs in the area of the protection of monuments and therefore has a direct impact on the selection of persons to whom supervision posts are entrusted. A novelty is the creation of the National Monument Protection Fund. It will be a special-purpose fund, the responsibility of which is the Minister of Culture and National Heritage. The revenue of the fund is derived from revenue from administrative fines. It is an additional financial mechanism supporting the owners of monuments. The amount of administrative fines is justified by the need to ensure effective protection of monuments, ranging from PLN 500 to PLN 500 000. zł. These penalties are described in detail in the draft law. The highest fines were imposed for conducting without permission or not in accordance with the terms or conditions set out in the licence of conservation works, restaurant works at the monument included in the Heritage Treasury List. It is the voivodship monument conservator that will be equipped with the possibility to adjudicate on administrative fines for violation of obligations related to the care of the monument. The provisions of the Act on Protected Monuments have also been adapted to introduce administrative fines for the illegal search for monuments, including archaeological monuments. Another amendment implies that the provisions of the Act on Protected Nature and the Act on the Protection of Monuments and the Protection of Monuments, which concern the removal of trees and shrubs from real estate sites entered in the Register of Monuments, are consistent. As a result, instead of issuing two permits to cut down trees or shrubs, one permit will be issued under the Act on the Protection of Monuments and the Protection of Monuments. It was proposed that the register of land and buildings should include information on the initiation of proceedings on the registration of the immovable property in the register. In this way, the person interested in acquiring the property will have access to information as to whether the object is not covered by the proceedings on the entry of the monument in the register of monuments. Many of the changes made to the bill are justified. Many of us found it difficult to agree, especially with high financial penalties. However, given the discussion in committee, questions, responses from representatives of the Ministry and the amendments made, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will be in favour of the bill amending the Act on the Protection of Monuments and the Protection of Monuments and some other laws. Thank you. [[Applause]]

When presenting the annual reporting documents to the authorities of the State, the National Council wishes to highlight the specificities of 2016 as this year concerns the report. It was an exceptional year in the activities of the National Radio and Television Council because it was the year of the change of term. The term of office of the previous National Radio and Television Council, chaired by Mr Jan Dworak, ended in six years. The second element... I would just like to say that the national council as a state body operates on a continuous basis, so that this report is a report that does not show this change, because also the tasks that are imposed by the legislator on the National Council of Radiophony and Television are tasks that are carried out continuously and in the same way. Thus, changes in the report itself are not visible, but in other documents, as in concrete decisions or strategic documents, these changes are sometimes very radical. I immediately answer the question: Is this continuity, continuation, or evolution? I will say: office - continuation, approach when it comes to dealing with many problems - sometimes 180 degrees back. This is all within the framework of the same legal situation, i.e. the existing law on radio and television. As regards legal situations, the legal environment, 2016 was also groundbreaking, because on 8 January the amendment of the Act on Radio and Television adopted on 30 December came into force. This amendment was adopted on 30 December 2015. This was the law which introduced changes in the area of competence of the National Radio and Television Council. These changes concerned inter alia. the right to appoint and dismiss boards and supervisory boards of public media companies. This power was initially conferred on the State Treasury Minister and was subsequently entrusted to the newly established National Media Council, which elects the members of the boards and supervisory boards of public media companies by means of a competition. As regards legal conditions, the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 13 December 2016, which ruled that the amendment law of 30 December 2015 was incompatible with the Constitution, should be cited here. The Court stressed that the National Radiophony and Television Council must have a dominant position in the framework of the market model developed by the legislator in the process of establishing public media companies. The relevant proposals for amending the law will probably be the subject of Parliament's deliberations. Ladies and gentlemen, the report presented today covers all the activities of the National Council. In particular, it shows, in it, the concession procedures that took place in 2016. Let me remind you that this is the year of launching the eighth television multiplex, with huge problems to this day. There is also information on how to perform other statutory tasks of the National Radio and Television Council, control over the activities of broadcasters, division of subscription fees, organisation and initiation of national cooperation. I would like to remind you that 2016 is about stepping up the European Commission's work on amending the audiovisual directive, adapting it to the new challenges of new technology, including the distribution of video content into the regulatory scope of the European countries. Poland has been intensively involved in this work, the national council, also under my leadership, has also been intensively involved in consulting and developing the position of the Polish government in relation to this directive. The Directive is at the level of further arrangements between Parliament, the Commission and the Council of the Union, so it will probably be adopted next year and we will have implementation work to do later. In 2016, there was also ongoing concerted work, consulting on the implementation by the Polish party, by the Polish government of the rules on the provision of public aid to public media, i.e. the principles related to the implementation of the mission, financed by the subscription. This is the implementation, the introduction of the so-called. public media duty cards. These works were carried out intensively in 2016, they are being conducted now, hosted by the Ministry of Culture. The National Council took part in the drafting of the position and in consultations and negotiations with representatives of the European Commission. Another important area of legal change was the attempt in 2016 to modernise the Radio and Television Act itself. Let me remind you that media law is over 20 years old. These changes that have been made today are also not sufficient. During this time, the media landscape changed dramatically and the need for such an amendment is very large. The National Council in the previous composition participated in the preparation of such a project, this project was considered, but it did not become the subject of a permanent government committee chaired by Prime Minister Kopacz at the time, and therefore after the elections, after the creation of a new government, this consultation path must be repeated. So we decided, because there are a lot of disagreements and views about the current composition of the national council, about the solutions proposed there, that we will rewrite the update of the Radio and Television Act. Ladies and gentlemen! The National Radio and Television Council signs with broadcasters financial-program agreements, financial-program plans, settles public broadcasters in the implementation of the mission, distributes money from the subscription. Because the issue of subscription is not strictly related to the report... Of course, in the report we have extensive information, very detailed how the subscription was shared. So that this image is also a picture presented in the report of the National Radio and Television Council. Ladies and gentlemen! We proceed with the examination of item 11 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the law on housing cooperatives and some other laws (print No 1624). I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction of Mr Kazimierz Smolinski to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u473	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Government's draft law amending the Housing Cooperatives Act and some other laws, print No 1624, primarily aims to align the provisions of the Housing Cooperatives Act with the judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal, to improve the operation of the cooperatives and to implement the intentions and objectives set out in the National Housing Program adopted by the Government last year. The draft contains the provisions adopted, proposed, adapting the cooperative law to the judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal. The first judgment of 14 February 2012, i.e. more than 5 years ago, found that the Housing Cooperatives Act was incompatible with the Constitution to the extent that it granted the tenant a claim for the transfer of ownership of the cooperative dwelling, which, with the free acquisition by the housing cooperative, was so-called. an old establishment exclusively linked to the obligation to repay the debt for the benefit arising from the lease. The bill makes it possible to buy out the so-called. former premises acquired free of charge by cooperatives after payment of the necessary and useful expenditure incurred by the cooperative. A further judgment of 5 February 2015 concerned the incompatibility of the Act with the Constitution in so far as it allows the membership of a housing cooperative of entities which do not have a legal title to the premises. The project provides for the granting of membership in the cooperative to persons bound by the law of the cooperative, i.e. having the right to a place or an expetition of that right. The second ruling of 2013 concerned non-compliance with the Constitution, in so far as housing cooperatives are authorised to regulate in the Statute the accounting rules for the return of the housing contribution where the dwelling is not sold by tender. The project provides for and introduces the principle of the valorisation of the housing contribution when, after the expiry of the cooperative tenant right to the premises, it is not possible to tender for the disposal of the premises. A further judgment of 2015 concerned, in turn, the non-compliance of the law with the Constitution as regards the failure to settle the renovation fund after the establishment of the housing community. The project introduces the principle of accounting by the cooperative with the owners of the premises of the established housing community of the registered income and expenditure of the repair fund. The project also provides for solutions to improve the activities of cooperatives, including: shall ensure that members of the cooperative are given the opportunity to participate in the general meeting by a representative, with the restriction that one representative may represent only one member. So far, there has been no such obligation, and many cooperatives in their statutes have banned the granting of power of attorney. In our view, they were registered by the courts even at that time. We introduce this possibility by law so that there is no doubt. The way in which votes are counted at the general meeting is also changing. The resolution of the General Meeting shall be adopted if the total number of members voting on the resolution is to be voted for by the Act or Statute. Changes also apply to general assemblies. They aim to improve the activities of this body by enabling the owners of premises created by the law of the housing community to decide on the management of joint real estate, whether it is still a cooperative or a community. Here members of the cooperative are left free. They do not have to be automatically deprived of the possibility of remaining and administering in the cooperative, as so far. The project also regulates the participation of owners of premises after the establishment of a housing community in expenditure related to the operation, the maintenance of real estate constituting the property of cooperatives intended to be used jointly by persons living in specific buildings or housing estates starting from the establishment of the community. It also exempts from enforcement media, electricity, water and gas charges paid by people living in housing cooperative resources. This is a revision of the Civil Procedure Code Act. Currently, these charges are subject to enforcement, which often results in the backlog of cooperatives towards media providers, aggravates the situation of cooperatives themselves, as well as all cooperatives, even those not in debt. The project also eliminates the possibility for the presidents to mirror the housing cooperative and modifies the powers of the Minister vis-à-vis the housing cooperative. We introduce a ban, we propose a ban so that the presidents of the cooperative and the persons who are directly subject to those presidents are not able to carry out the functions associated with the illustration as set out in the Act. They have to decide either they want to be president or they want to be a illustrator. Unfortunately, the practice is that the presidents who act as presidents - this is due to complaints that affect the Ministry - these illegible illustrations are carried out. We assume that when this chapter takes place that the illustrators will be persons who are not co-operatives, I apologize, the presidents of the cooperative, these mirrors will be carried out more honestly. As regards the modification of the powers of the Minister for Housing or Construction towards housing cooperatives, this is a slight change here. Of course, we must respect cooperatives as a kind of private, partly private, partly common property, so this intervention of the Minister cannot be too big, but at present there is virtually no control, i.e. is limited only to obtaining explanations from cooperatives concerning complaints. We are introducing the possibility that the Minister will be able to obtain or demand the presentation of documents that may be examined. On the basis of these documents, it will be possible to have a mirror carried out or to make a notification, e.g. law enforcement authorities if it is established that there is a suspicion of a crime. The project also includes these solutions, which I was talking about when it comes to the National Housing Programme, because there are barriers in the cooperative residential rights segment, these are new cooperative investments. The cooperative will be able to set a time limit after which it will be possible to transfer the ownership of the premises where it is possible to introduce this tenant right, typical to preserve the nature and structure of the cooperative at the moment. Practically in recent years, cooperatives have ceased to build. The ones they're building are actually just a developer, that is, they're building for sale or for rent. Most people don't. We want cooperatives to be able to achieve their basic goal, namely to build for their members. If they make an appointment with a person who wishes to be a member of the cooperative, in due course this property may be transferred if there is no funding from the state budget. Where there will be budget funding, this transfer will not be possible, but where it will be market financing, this transfer will be possible. And these are actually all the main principles of the law. I ask the High House to process and adopt the proposed amendment to the Housing Cooperatives Act. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u481	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform, I have the honour to present a position on the government draft law amending the Housing Cooperatives Act and some other laws contained in print no. 1624. The draft amendment concerns the matter covered by the judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal and seeks to implement it by adapting the provisions of the Housing Cooperatives Act of 15 December 2000 to the three Constitutional Tribunal judgments: 14 February 2012, sygn. Act P 17/10, 27 June 2013, sygn. Act K 36/12, and of 5 February 2015, sygn. Act K 60/13. However, the draft also contains regulations in areas where the Constitutional Tribunal did not comment, and these are the most controversial. In general, the amendments implementing the Constitutional Court's judgments are always positive, as they remove legal uncertainty, while detailed solutions are always the legislator's. I'll try to describe them briefly. There are changes in the scope of the article. 3 The Housing Cooperatives Act is the consequence of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal in Case K 60/13, in which the Constitutional Tribunal expressly stated that the members of the cooperative may be persons who have an interest in meeting the housing needs, i.e. those who have a cooperative tenant's right to an establishment, a cooperative ownership right to an establishment, a right to a separate ownership or an exhibition of a separate ownership of the premises. This change does not seem to give rise to any doubt, as it is a consequence of the judgment of the Constitutional Court. One can only think about why a 4-year membership period for founders, which, if the right to a place is not established, expires and why it is 4 years. Amendments to Article 11 are, on the other hand, a consequence of the judgment of the Constitutional Court in Case K 36/12. These changes regulate the problems so far in situations where, after the apartment has been released, the apartment itself is not sold by tender. Indeed, this case has been left out in the present law, and it is important to regulate such situations. It is also important that these regulations appear in the Act and not only in the Statute, and relate to the settlement rules for the reimbursement of the housing contribution equivalent to the cooperative tenant's right to the premises in a situation where the premises cannot be disposed of. However, some other records are not entirely understandable. First and foremost, there is no indication of what the cooperative is going to do with such an empty premises. Sometimes it comes from other regulations, and sometimes it doesn't, e.g. when the premises are built with the contribution of the National Housing Fund. Lips are also unclear. 2 concerning the condition for a housing contribution and the conclusion of an agreement to establish a cooperative tenant right to the premises referred to in paragraph 2. 2. So further work is needed on the project. In contrast, an amendment to the Article. 48 of the Act is the consequence of the judgment of the Constitutional Court in Case P 27/10, which the previous regulation found incompatible with Article. Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Constitution because of infringement of the property rights of the cooperative. The solution is therefore less favourable to the entitled, but in line with the constitution. This amendment regulates the issue of residential housing which has been taken over by housing cooperatives. Until now, the tenant had only a duty to repay the debt for the benefits arising from the lease agreement, so it cannot be said that this case was not settled because it was settled. Now, on the other hand, the novel amends and adds to the previous debt the need to pay the costs incurred by the cooperative for the necessary expenditures, where the cooperative purchased the building free of charge, or to pay the amount determined by the management of the cooperative, resulting from the market value of the purchase price of the building, where the cooperative purchased the building for payment. Unfortunately, there are also some legal loopholes in the draft, in which the Constitutional Tribunal did not comment. I mean, in particular, the draft article. 93a Cooperative law, which is the next step in strengthening democratic centralism by the current authority. The change is the introduction of one word, which changes the whole meaning of this article and the law as such. The fundamental difference is that in § 2 is not a case of infringement, but a case of suspected infringement, and that is a fundamental difference, because, as you know, suspicions can always be, especially if someone has a suspicious nature. This amendment introduces extraordinary powers for the Minister, who may, in the event of a suspicion of a breach of law itself, apply for an examination. It doesn't seem right. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Therefore, the Civic Platform proposes to submit a draft law amending the Housing Cooperatives Act for further processing in the Infrastructure Commission. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Since 2000, Poland has seen one of the biggest increases in the skills of 15-year-olds in PISA research. The biggest leap in the results of these studies was achieved after the first edition of PISA in 2000, which included students before the 1999 reform, i.e. high school students, technicians and vocational schools. The additional year of secondary education had a particularly positive impact on former vocational school pupils. This impact is sustainable and at present vocational school pupils aged 16, 17 have higher skills than pupils in these schools prior to the introduction of secondary education. Both Polish and international studies show that the achievements of Polish 15-year-olds are above the OECD average and the European Union average. In the natural sciences Poles took the tenth place in the 26 countries of the European Union that participated in the study, in the reading we took the fifth place, in mathematics - the eighth. In the European Union, only six countries achieved better results in natural sciences, and only the students of Finland, Ireland and Estonia achieved better results in reading. Poland is less affluent than most European countries, and Polish families have on average lower education and lower cultural resources. After taking into account socio-economic factors, Poland's performance in natural sciences shifts us from 10th to 4th place in the European Union. Young Poles have skills higher than the European average. Let me repeat - the record holder will be teaching since September in seven schools in Podkarpacie. How much time will that teacher have for the student, how much time will the students have in contact with that teacher? The fifth lie: it is citizens and citizens who want these changes and we are ready for dialogue. Yes, two years ago, education reform plans were supported by most of the population, but when we learned the details, it turned out that some of us did not expect what the Ministry of National Education proposed, and support began to decline. However, the ministry remained deaf to the voices of criticism and even to ordinary questions. Minister Anna Zalewska often talked about dialogue, consultations, the fact that everyone can comment, and the Ministry is open to dialogue and questions, but how did this dialogue with representatives look in practice? Minister Anna Zalewska has never met with representatives of parental movements opposing the reform. During the past year he once met with his parents Deputy Minister Maciej Kopeć - in order to inform them that the mode of reform is conditional on the electoral calendar and no changes in its shape or at the rate of implementation can take place. Putting the electoral calendar above the welfare of the child leaves no comment. [[Applause]] In December, before voting on the Law of Education, parents sent a letter to all 231 Members of Law and Justice. The sixth lie: reform is costless. The way in which the reform was implemented surprises Deputy Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki. He was disappointed by the analysis of the costs of the changes made when he assessed the proposal for a new regulation on framework teaching plans. According to the officials of Prime Minister Morawiecki, the costs of education reform were to be paid by local governments, but it turns out that the government will have to find additional resources. The cost of who or what? The foundation of the Law of 14 December 2016. Educational law introducing reform of the school system was that any change in the educational tasks of local government units would be financed by their funds, including from the educational part of the subsidy, without the need to increase it. The provisions of the proposed regulation are a consequence of this reform - wrote the Ministry of Development and Finance in its opinion. The Ministry was also surprised by the MEN's calculations of the costs of the changes, as the reform was to bring savings for the education system in the years to come. What kind of savings? Since the creation of junior high schools, i.e. since 1999, local governments have spent PLN 130 billion on junior high school, including PLN 8 billion on investment. The cost of losing junior high only in the first 2 years is estimated at PLN 1 billion. It is difficult to estimate the costs of dismissals and briefings of teachers and school administration staff and the cost of renovating and adapting school infrastructure. That's what local governments will pay for. It is about the money to buy benches or chairs for the youngest students to those primary schools that will be built in place of junior high schools, as well as the purchase of lighthouse equipment. In addition, there is the cost of archiving the documentation of the schools that have been removed and the cost of purchasing new seals and plates, the fitting of school cafés, the adaptation of sanitary facilities to the needs of the youngest children. Let's look at examples of expenditure this year: Bydgoszcz - PLN 3 million, Gdańsk - 25 million, Warsaw - 65 million, Poznań - 13 million. And these are just a few cities. We all know the government doesn't have its own money. That's our money. We can see how wasted they are. Self-governments already write applications for funding to the Ministry of National Education in case the secondary school is converted into an 8-year primary school. The first requests concerning the renovation of the cemetery were submitted by 330 local authorities. They account for over PLN 17 million. As far as sanitary renovations are concerned, 308 applications for more than 32 million PLN have been received in this respect. Towns such as Warsaw, Lublin, Wroclaw and Katowice have been the biggest beneficiaries. The cost of printing textbooks subsidised from the budget only in 2017 is 357 million. But they're gonna be out of date, and money's gonna be wasted. With this money you can build daycare, kindergartens, gymnasiums in schools where they're missing them. The whole society will pay for reckless changes, including those who don't have children. [[Applause]] And the last lie: the reform is already in place and it cannot be reversed. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-20-sejm-44-1.u720	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you. I would like to present to you a little known history of the beginnings of one of the best cancer treatment facilities in Poland - the Institute im. Maria Skłodowska-Curie Centre for Oncology in Gliwice, and to pay close attention to the almost forgotten figure of Mr Stanisław Bylina, who first in 1939 in Chorzów, and finally in 1946 in Gliwice created the seed of an anticancer institute. The future director of the institute was born on 8 May 1903 in Unii. There he graduated from elementary school and started his junior high school. The outbreak of the First World War and later of the Polish-Bolshevik War caused the study to continue in Vienna and finally submitted a maturity exam in Poznań. At the university there, he graduated with the result of a medical degree aimed at him, and less than a year later he received a doctorate in all medical sciences. As an assistant at the university surgical clinic, he also took biological studies. In 1930, he learned how to fight cancer in London under the guidance of the pioneer radiotherapy Stanford Cade and in Stockholm, Paris and Brussels. In 1932, he founded the first modern cancer center in Poland and a cancer ward in the Poznań clinic. A unique study was conducted in the field of X-rays, radiotherapy, and histopathology. Soon the facility became the first comprehensive oncology unit. In 1938, he took action to create a cancer clinic in Upper Silesia. Brackets hospital in Chorzów was chosen for its headquarters, and Dr. Stanisław Bylina was the organizer and future manager. Several days before the Germans entered, the opening of the institute was already within reach. After mobilization from 1 to 25 September, he continued to serve in the lead surgical command of the seventh corps district and then improvised group of the Polish Army Dubno. After a failed attempt to break into Hungary and capitulate the group, he was captured. During the occupation he first served in a POW hospital and then in a hospital organized with the consent of the German authorities of the clinic in Jarosław. In 1940 he became a soldier of the ZWZ-AK Jarosław circuit, and his clinic became a hospital of the Rzeszów suburb. Despite helping many underground soldiers and fleeing prisoners and Jews, he escaped arrest. After the Red Army entered, it was directed to work in the established public health service. In February 1945, he appeared as a soldier of the Home Army and went to Poznań, where he was recovering from the diseases he had suffered during the German occupation. In the spring of 1946, after writing a memorial to the Ministry of Health on the reconstruction of the anti-cancer institute in Chorzów, Stanisław Bylin was entrusted with directing the organization and movement of the institute, placing it in Gliwice. The site was inaugurated in July 1947. Thanks to his efforts until the end of the 1940s, the Gliwice Institute was equipped with state-of-the-art equipment and equipment. Since 1948, as part of the progressive Stalinization, Director Stanisław Bylina began attacking, first building a political chase around him, and in 1950 falsely accusing medical errors and mismanagement of the facility. Although the accusations were absurd, the case continued until 1953. In the meantime, he was fired from the institute and was ordered to work as a laryngologist in the work clinic in Leszno. After the so-called. thawing in 1956, he was stopped being persecuted and allowed to move to Warsaw, and nine years later he was allowed to return to the surgeon's specialty. Stanisław Bylina died on June 26, 1976 and was buried at the Military Cemetery in Powązki. Thank you.
